Whether the code at node may run as strict code: the file may be an ES module, which is strict code throughout whatever its text says, or the text makes it strict.

Expand source code Browse git 
def may_run_as_strict(self, node: Node) -> bool:
    """
    Whether the code at *node* may run as strict code: the file may be an ES module, which is
    strict code throughout whatever its text says, or the text makes it strict.
    """
    return self.file_kind.may_be_an_es_module or strict_mode_at(node)
def annex_b_copy_observed(self, declaration)

Whether anything may observe the copy Annex B makes of the function declaration, declared in a block, or the var that copy declares: the var is read, written, named in a with body, exported, carried by an object handed to a call, reachable by reflection, or reached by code outside the file (reached_from_outside), as a browser navigates once the copy stores a function declared in a block at the top of a script under the name location. A write observes the var because without it the same assignment lands on whatever the name means outside the function, or creates a global. A declaration this model was not built over may be observed.

Expand source code Browse git 
def annex_b_copy_observed(self, declaration: JsFunctionDeclaration) -> bool:
    """
    Whether anything may observe the copy Annex B makes of the function *declaration*, declared
    in a block, or the `var` that copy declares: the `var` is read, written, named in a `with`
    body, exported, carried by an object handed to a call, reachable by reflection, or reached
    by code outside the file (`reached_from_outside`), as a browser navigates once the copy
    stores a function declared in a block at the top of a script under the name `location`. A
    write observes the `var` because without it the same assignment lands on whatever the name
    means outside the function, or creates a global. A declaration this model was not built
    over may be observed.
    """
    if declaration.id is None:
        return False
    block = self.binding_of(declaration.id)
    if block is None:
        return True
    return any(
        copy.observed
        or copy.var.writes
        or copy.var.has_indefinite_write
        or self.reflection_can_reach(copy.var)
        or self.reached_from_outside(copy.var)
        for copy in block.copies_made
        if copy.declaration is declaration
    )
def declaration_may_land(self, names, scope, *, exclude=())

Whether declarations of names may be introduced directly in scope: none of them captures a use already meaningful there (would_capture), and none lands on a binding the host supplies (Binding.supplied_by_host), which no identifier of the text spells for that walk to find. At the top of a CommonJS file a let of a parameter's name makes Node refuse the file as CommonJS, and a var of it starts out holding what the host passed rather than undefined.

None lands on a property of the global object a host reads (lands_on_the_host) either: a Function body declaring var onload and spliced at the top would install a load handler the window then calls once more. A pass that can give such a declaration another name asks lands_on_the_host first and asks here about the name it chose.

Expand source code Browse git 
def declaration_may_land(
    self,
    names: set[str],
    scope: Scope,
    *,
    exclude: Collection[Binding] = (),
) -> bool:
    """
    Whether declarations of *names* may be introduced directly in *scope*: none of them captures
    a use already meaningful there (`would_capture`), and none lands on a binding the host
    supplies (`Binding.supplied_by_host`), which no identifier of the text spells for that walk
    to find. At the top of a CommonJS file a `let` of a parameter's name makes Node refuse the
    file as CommonJS, and a `var` of it starts out holding what the host passed rather than
    `undefined`.

    None lands on a property of the global object a host reads (`lands_on_the_host`) either: a
    `Function` body declaring `var onload` and spliced at the top would install a load handler
    the window then calls once more. A pass that can give such a declaration another name asks
    `lands_on_the_host` first and asks here about the name it chose.
    """
    if any(self.lands_on_the_host(name, scope) for name in names):
        return False
    for name in names:
        binding = scope.bindings.get(name)
        if binding is not None and binding.supplied_by_host:
            return False
    return not self.would_capture(names, scope, exclude=exclude)
def would_capture(self, names, scope, *, exclude=())

Whether introducing a binding for any of names directly in scope would capture an identifier already meaningful there. Every use-position occurrence of one of names within scope, including in a nested function that would close over the new binding, must already resolve to a binding strictly nested below scope (see is_shadowed); otherwise that occurrence — free, inherited from an enclosing scope, or bound in scope itself — would be rebound by the introduced declaration.

A use resolving to a binding in exclude is skipped: the edit the caller asks this for deletes that binding with the same splice that introduces the declaration, so the use is carried off rather than captured. The natural binding is what decides membership — a resolution walked past an excluded binding answers where the name would land after the edit, which is the question the callers below _admit_reflected_body ask of their free names, not the one this capture rule asks of a use the tree still holds.

Code a string spells uses names no identifier of the text holds, and a declaration introduced where that code looks a name up captures that use as well (_captured_in_spelled_code). No edit carries such a use off, so exclude does not apply to it.

Expand source code Browse git 
def would_capture(
    self, names: set[str], scope: Scope, *, exclude: Collection[Binding] = (),
) -> bool:
    """
    Whether introducing a binding for any of *names* directly in *scope* would capture an
    identifier already meaningful there. Every use-position occurrence of one of *names* within
    *scope*, including in a nested function that would close over the new binding, must already
    resolve to a binding strictly nested below *scope* (see `is_shadowed`); otherwise that
    occurrence — free, inherited from an enclosing scope, or bound in *scope* itself — would be
    rebound by the introduced declaration.

    A use resolving to a binding in *exclude* is skipped: the edit the caller asks this for
    deletes that binding with the same splice that introduces the declaration, so the use is
    carried off rather than captured. The natural binding is what decides membership — a
    resolution walked past an excluded binding answers where the name would land *after* the
    edit, which is the question the callers below `_admit_reflected_body` ask of their free
    names, not the one this capture rule asks of a use the tree still holds.

    Code a string spells uses names no identifier of the text holds, and a declaration
    introduced where that code looks a name up captures that use as well
    (`_captured_in_spelled_code`). No edit carries such a use off, so *exclude* does not apply
    to it.
    """
    if self._captured_in_spelled_code(names, scope):
        return True
    return next(self._capturing_uses(names, scope, exclude), None) is not None
def captured_names(self, names, scope, *, exclude=())

Those of names for which would_capture holds, read in one walk of scope.

Expand source code Browse git 
def captured_names(
    self, names: set[str], scope: Scope, *, exclude: Collection[Binding] = (),
) -> set[str]:
    """
    Those of *names* for which `would_capture` holds, read in one walk of *scope*.
    """
    captured = {node.name for node in self._capturing_uses(names, scope, exclude)}
    return captured | self._captured_in_spelled_code(names, scope)
def has_reflection_surface(self)

Whether the program still contains a construct through which code could reference a global by name at runtime: a value-read of the eval or Function intrinsic in any form — a direct or indirect call, an alias (var e = eval), a comma sequence ((0, eval)), or a member access (window.eval, g['Function']) — a string-valued timer, a dynamic property read on the global object (window[expr]), a with statement, or a span of source this model never read, which may spell a name nothing here records. Computed conservatively (over-reporting is safe): while any such surface remains, a dead global must not be removed, because reflective code may read it. A computed global write names no global (has_opaque_global_write owns that question) and is not counted here.

Expand source code Browse git 
def has_reflection_surface(self) -> bool:
    """
    Whether the program still contains a construct through which code could reference a global
    by name at runtime: a value-read of the `eval` or `Function` intrinsic in any form — a
    direct or indirect call, an alias (`var e = eval`), a comma sequence (`(0, eval)`), or a
    member access (`window.eval`, `g['Function']`) — a string-valued timer, a dynamic property
    read on the global object (`window[expr]`), a `with` statement, or a span of source this
    model never read, which may spell a name nothing here records. Computed conservatively
    (over-reporting is safe): while any such surface remains, a dead global must not be removed,
    because reflective code may read it. A computed global *write* names no global
    (`has_opaque_global_write` owns that question) and is not counted here.
    """
    self._ensure_reflection_detected()
    assert self._reflection_surface is not None
    return self._reflection_surface
def reached_without_its_name(self, function)

Whether function may be called, constructed or handed out where no reference of a name holding it stands: the program may read the caller of a function (spells_caller), which is whichever function called the one it is read on; the body may reach the function through its own arguments (arguments_name_their_function); or the body reads new.target (references_new_target), which is the function itself wherever new runs it. The one answer every reader asking how a function may be invoked or obtained shares, memoized per function.

Expand source code Browse git 
def reached_without_its_name(self, function: Node) -> bool:
    """
    Whether *function* may be called, constructed or handed out where no reference of a name
    holding it stands: the program may read the `caller` of a function (`spells_caller`), which
    is whichever function called the one it is read on; the body may reach the function through
    its own `arguments` (`arguments_name_their_function`); or the body reads `new.target`
    (`references_new_target`), which is the function itself wherever `new` runs it. The one
    answer every reader asking how a function may be invoked or obtained shares, memoized per
    function.
    """
    cached = self._reached_unnamed.get(function)
    if cached is None:
        cached = self._reached_unnamed[function] = (
            self.spells_caller()
            or arguments_name_their_function(function)
            or references_new_target(function)
        )
    return cached
def spells_caller(self)

Whether the program may read the caller of a function: it spells the name as a property it reads or destructures, in a string, or as a name that no binding of the program holds, which a with body may resolve to a property. In sloppy mode g.caller is whichever function called g, so such a program can reach every function that calls another one without naming it. A variable named caller and a key of that name in an object literal or a class read no such property. Memoized.

Expand source code Browse git 
def spells_caller(self) -> bool:
    """
    Whether the program may read the `caller` of a function: it spells the name as a property
    it reads or destructures, in a string, or as a name that no binding of the program holds,
    which a `with` body may resolve to a property. In sloppy mode `g.caller` is whichever
    function called `g`, so such a program can reach every function that calls another one
    without naming it. A variable named `caller` and a key of that name in an object literal or
    a class read no such property. Memoized.
    """
    if self._spells_caller is None:
        self._spells_caller = any(self._may_read_caller_at(node) for node in self.root.walk())
    return self._spells_caller
def host_reaches_global(self, name)

Whether a host reads what the file stores under name from outside the file, in one of two ways. The host environment reads the property name of the global object: it calls the event handler stored there, navigates to the address stored there, or keeps it for the next page (HostEnvironment.reads_stored_value()). Or the analyst declared the name one a host calls by name (_names_an_entrypoint), which finds what global code finds under it. A question about a binding asks named_by_the_host, which tells the two apart.

Expand source code Browse git 
def host_reaches_global(self, name: str) -> bool:
    """
    Whether a host reads what the file stores under *name* from outside the file, in one of two
    ways. The host *environment* reads the property *name* of the global object: it calls the
    event handler stored there, navigates to the address stored there, or keeps it for the next
    page (`refinery.lib.scripts.js.analysis.environment.HostEnvironment.reads_stored_value`).
    Or the analyst declared the name one a host calls by name (`_names_an_entrypoint`), which
    finds what global code finds under it. A question about a binding asks `named_by_the_host`,
    which tells the two apart.
    """
    return self.environment.reads_stored_value(name) or self._names_an_entrypoint(name)
def lands_on_the_host(self, name, scope)

Whether a var or function declaration of name in scope would be a property of the global object the host has a part in: scope is the top scope of a script, and the host reads the property (host_reaches_global) or holds it before the script runs (HostEnvironment.holds()). A declaration that lands there in place of one that bound a name of its own body stores into what the host reads, or reads what the host put there. A let there shadows it, or is refused with a SyntaxError where the property cannot be redefined.

Expand source code Browse git 
def lands_on_the_host(self, name: str, scope: Scope) -> bool:
    """
    Whether a `var` or function declaration of *name* in *scope* would be a property of the
    global object the host has a part in: *scope* is the top scope of a script, and the host
    reads the property (`host_reaches_global`) or holds it before the script runs
    (`refinery.lib.scripts.js.analysis.environment.HostEnvironment.holds`). A declaration that
    lands there in place of one that bound a name of its own body stores into what the host
    reads, or reads what the host put there. A `let` there shadows it, or is refused with a
    `SyntaxError` where the property cannot be redefined.
    """
    return (
        scope is self.root_scope
        and not self.file_kind.scopes_declarations_to_the_file
        and (self.host_reaches_global(name) or self.environment.holds(name))
    )
def named_by_the_host(self, binding)

Whether a host may reach binding by name: the host environment reads the property of the global object under that name (HostEnvironment.reads_stored_value()) and the binding is that property (_is_the_global_property), or the host calls it by name (called_by_name). A declared object is reached as much as a declared function, and the host converts a value stored in location or name to a string, which calls the methods it carries.

Expand source code Browse git 
def named_by_the_host(self, binding: Binding) -> bool:
    """
    Whether a host may reach *binding* by name: the host *environment* reads the property of
    the global object under that name
    (`refinery.lib.scripts.js.analysis.environment.HostEnvironment.reads_stored_value`) and the
    binding is that property (`_is_the_global_property`), or the host calls it by name
    (`called_by_name`). A declared object is reached as much as a declared function, and the
    host converts a value stored in `location` or `name` to a string, which calls the methods it
    carries.
    """
    if self.environment.reads_stored_value(binding.name) and self._is_the_global_property(binding):
        return True
    return self.called_by_name(binding)
def kept_by_the_host(self, binding)

Whether binding is a property of the global object that keeps a value of its own when the script stores one: the host holds it before the script runs (HostEnvironment.keeps_its_own_value()), such as the status of a browser window, which keeps a string, or its top, which keeps the window, and the binding is a var at the top of a script or the one Annex B gives a function declared in a block there, neither of which declares a property of its own, or a name the script never declares. A store into such a name also finds a property the global object inherits, such as the self of a dedicated worker, which has no setter (HostEnvironment.inherits_a_property_keeping_its_own_value()); a var declares a property of its own in front of that one. What a read of it gives is not what the file stored. A function declared at the top of the script replaces such a property with a variable of its own, where the copy Annex B makes of one declared in a block stores into it.

Expand source code Browse git 
def kept_by_the_host(self, binding: Binding) -> bool:
    """
    Whether *binding* is a property of the global object that keeps a value of its own when the
    script stores one: the host holds it before the script runs
    (`refinery.lib.scripts.js.analysis.environment.HostEnvironment.keeps_its_own_value`), such
    as the `status` of a browser window, which keeps a string, or its `top`, which keeps the
    window, and the binding is a `var` at the top of a script or the one Annex B gives a
    function declared in a block there, neither of which declares a property of its own, or a
    name the script never declares. A store into such a name also finds a property the global
    object inherits, such as the `self` of a dedicated worker, which has no setter
    (`refinery.lib.scripts.js.analysis.environment.HostEnvironment.inherits_a_property_keeping_its_own_value`);
    a `var` declares a property of its own in front of that one. What a read of it gives is not
    what the file stored. A function declared at the top of the script replaces such a property
    with a variable of its own, where the copy Annex B makes of one declared in a block stores
    into it.
    """
    environment = self.environment
    return (
        binding.kind in (BindingKind.VAR, BindingKind.FUNCTION, BindingKind.IMPLICIT_GLOBAL)
        and (
            environment.keeps_its_own_value(binding.name)
            or binding.kind is BindingKind.IMPLICIT_GLOBAL
            and environment.inherits_a_property_keeping_its_own_value(binding.name)
        )
        and self.reaches_global_object(binding)
        and not any(
            isinstance(function := declaration.parent, JsFunctionDeclaration)
            and (holder := statement_list_holding(function)) is not None
            and hoists_its_functions(holder)
            for declaration in binding.declarations
        )
    )
def called_by_the_host(self, binding)

Whether a host may call the function binding holds: the host environment calls the event handler stored in the property of the global object under that name (HostEnvironment.calls_handler()), and the binding is that property (_is_the_global_property), as a window calls its onload; or the host calls it by name (called_by_name).

Expand source code Browse git 
def called_by_the_host(self, binding: Binding) -> bool:
    """
    Whether a host may call the function *binding* holds: the host *environment* calls the
    event handler stored in the property of the global object under that name
    (`refinery.lib.scripts.js.analysis.environment.HostEnvironment.calls_handler`), and the
    binding is that property (`_is_the_global_property`), as a window calls its `onload`; or
    the host calls it by name (`called_by_name`).
    """
    if self.environment.calls_handler(binding.name) and self._is_the_global_property(binding):
        return True
    return self.called_by_name(binding)
def called_by_name(self, binding)

Whether a host calls binding by its name, which the analyst declared one it calls (_names_an_entrypoint), and finds the binding there as global code does (global_code_finds), itself or through the var Annex B copies it into: a page's onclick="run()" finds a top-level let run of a script, which is no property of the global object.

Expand source code Browse git 
def called_by_name(self, binding: Binding) -> bool:
    """
    Whether a host calls *binding* by its name, which the analyst declared one it calls
    (`_names_an_entrypoint`), and finds the binding there as global code does
    (`global_code_finds`), itself or through the `var` Annex B copies it into: a page's
    `onclick="run()"` finds a top-level `let run` of a script, which is no property of the
    global object.
    """
    return self._names_an_entrypoint(binding.name) and _itself_or_its_annex_b_copy(
        binding, self.global_code_finds
    )
def reached_from_outside(self, binding)

Whether code outside the file may reach binding by name at some point through the global object, reading what it holds, keeping a value of its own in it, or calling it: a host reads it (named_by_the_host), the host keeps a value of its own in it (kept_by_the_host), or code outside the file calls it (called_from_outside). An importer reaching an export is not among them: that is Binding.exported, which a caller asks beside this. A pass that could delete such a declaration or a store into it, fold a read of one into its value, or relocate one out of the global scope asks here before doing so, so that the answer is one predicate's rather than each pass's own.

Expand source code Browse git 
def reached_from_outside(self, binding: Binding) -> bool:
    """
    Whether code outside the file may reach *binding* by name at some point through the global
    object, reading what it holds, keeping a value of its own in it, or calling it: a host reads
    it (`named_by_the_host`), the host keeps a value of its own in it (`kept_by_the_host`), or
    code outside the file calls it (`called_from_outside`). An importer reaching an export is
    not among them: that is `Binding.exported`, which a caller asks beside this. A pass that
    could delete such a declaration or a store into it, fold a read of one into its value, or
    relocate one out of the global scope asks here before doing so, so that the answer is one
    predicate's rather than each pass's own.
    """
    return (
        self.named_by_the_host(binding)
        or self.kept_by_the_host(binding)
        or self.called_from_outside(binding)
    )
def called_from_outside(self, binding)

Whether code outside the file may call the function binding holds, by its name through the global object: a host calls it (called_by_the_host), or the file is a library, holding nothing but function declarations at its top (declares_only_functions), and binding names one of them as a property of the global object, which code that loads the library calls once the file has run. A pass asking where or how often a function runs asks here; one asking whether what a name holds is read asks reached_from_outside.

Expand source code Browse git 
def called_from_outside(self, binding: Binding) -> bool:
    """
    Whether code outside the file may call the function *binding* holds, by its name through
    the global object: a host calls it (`called_by_the_host`), or the file is a library,
    holding nothing but function declarations at its top (`declares_only_functions`), and
    *binding* names one of them as a property of the global object, which code that loads the
    library calls once the file has run. A pass asking where or how often a function runs asks
    here; one asking whether what a name holds is read asks `reached_from_outside`.
    """
    if self.called_by_the_host(binding):
        return True
    return (
        self.declares_only_functions()
        and self.reaches_global_object(binding)
        and any(
            isinstance(function := declaration.parent, JsFunctionDeclaration)
            and function.parent is self.root
            for declaration in binding.declarations
        )
    )
def reached_without_a_read(self, binding)

Whether code may reach binding where no read of it in the file stands: an importer reads it as an export, the call writes it on entry (Binding.written_at_entry), a body the file does not read is handed the object carrying it (Binding.reachable_through_a_handed_object), reflection reaches it (reflection_can_reach), or code outside the file reaches it by name (reached_from_outside). A reader asking whether the reads of a name are every way to what it holds asks here.

Expand source code Browse git 
def reached_without_a_read(self, binding: Binding) -> bool:
    """
    Whether code may reach *binding* where no read of it in the file stands: an importer reads
    it as an export, the call writes it on entry (`Binding.written_at_entry`), a body the file
    does not read is handed the object carrying it
    (`Binding.reachable_through_a_handed_object`), reflection reaches it
    (`reflection_can_reach`), or code outside the file reaches it by name
    (`reached_from_outside`). A reader asking whether the reads of a name are every way to what
    it holds asks here.
    """
    return (
        binding.exported
        or binding.written_at_entry
        or binding.reachable_through_a_handed_object
        or self.reflection_can_reach(binding)
        or self.reached_from_outside(binding)
    )

Whether binding is exported from a module an importer can run in before its body has finished (module_may_be_reentered), so the importer can read it at a point the file's text does not order.

def importer_may_run_first(self, binding: Binding) -> bool:
    """
    Whether *binding* is exported from a module an importer can run in before its body has
    finished (`module_may_be_reentered`), so the importer can read it at a point the file's
    text does not order.
    """
    return binding.exported and self.module_may_be_reentered()
def callable_from_outside(self, function)

Whether code outside the file can call function while the file's own text still runs, at a point that text does not order: a host calls a function it names (called_by_the_host) whenever the script hands it control, and an importer in an import cycle calls an export, one exported by name or an anonymous export default function, before the module's body has finished (importer_may_run_first). The functions of a library (called_from_outside) are not among them, and neither are the exports an importer outside every cycle reaches: code that loads the file calls those once it has run.

Expand source code Browse git 
def callable_from_outside(self, function: Node) -> bool:
    """
    Whether code outside the file can call *function* while the file's own text still runs, at
    a point that text does not order: a host calls a function it names (`called_by_the_host`)
    whenever the script hands it control, and an importer in an import cycle calls an export,
    one exported by name or an anonymous `export default function`, before the module's body
    has finished (`importer_may_run_first`). The functions of a library
    (`called_from_outside`) are not among them, and neither are the exports an importer outside
    every cycle reaches: code that loads the file calls those once it has run.
    """
    names = self.function_names(function)
    for binding in () if names is None else names.bindings:
        if self.importer_may_run_first(binding) or self.called_by_the_host(binding):
            return True
    return (
        isinstance(function.parent, JsExportDefaultDeclaration)
        and self.module_may_be_reentered()
    )
def declares_only_functions(self)

Whether the top level of the file holds function declarations and nothing else. Memoized.

Expand source code Browse git 
def declares_only_functions(self) -> bool:
    """
    Whether the top level of the file holds function declarations and nothing else. Memoized.
    """
    if self._declares_only_functions is None:
        body = self.root.body
        self._declares_only_functions = bool(body) and all(
            isinstance(statement, JsFunctionDeclaration) for statement in body
        )
    return self._declares_only_functions
def constructions_may_be_hidden(self)

Whether a function the program constructs may be constructed with arguments no call the text shows passes it: a constructor may escape (constructors_may_escape), or the program constructs one without naming it (_constructs_unseen), through a constructor property or through new.target. Memoized.

Expand source code Browse git 
def constructions_may_be_hidden(self) -> bool:
    """
    Whether a function the program constructs may be constructed with arguments no call the
    text shows passes it: a constructor may escape (`constructors_may_escape`), or the program
    constructs one without naming it (`_constructs_unseen`), through a `constructor` property or
    through `new.target`. Memoized.
    """
    if self._constructions_hidden is None:
        self._constructions_hidden = self.constructors_may_escape() or any(
            _constructs_unseen(node, self.assumed_keys) for node in self.root.walk()
        )
    return self._constructions_hidden
def constructors_may_escape(self)

Whether a constructor may run on a receiver or with arguments of the program's choice: the program spells "constructor" in a string or in a concatenation of constant strings, reads a constructor property other than to construct with it, compare it, read its name or test it, or destructures one. An instance reaches the function that built it through that property, and so does its prototype. Memoized.

Expand source code Browse git 
def constructors_may_escape(self) -> bool:
    """
    Whether a constructor may run on a receiver or with arguments of the program's choice: the
    program spells `"constructor"` in a string or in a concatenation of constant strings, reads
    a `constructor` property other than to construct with it, compare it, read its name or test
    it, or destructures one. An instance reaches the function that built it through that
    property, and so does its prototype. Memoized.
    """
    if self._constructors_escape is None:
        concatenations: list[JsBinaryExpression] = []
        escapes = False
        for node in self.root.walk():
            if isinstance(node, JsBinaryExpression):
                if node.operator == '+':
                    concatenations.append(node)
            elif _lets_a_constructor_escape(node):
                escapes = True
                break
        self._constructors_escape = escapes or concatenation_spells(concatenations, 'constructor')
    return self._constructors_escape
def strings_still_unseen(self)

The names of STRINGS_READ_ANYWHERE whose readers answer no for this program, so that a string spelling one anywhere would change an answer: one spelling constructor lets a constructor escape (constructors_may_escape), and one spelling caller may read the function that called another (spells_caller).

Expand source code Browse git 
def strings_still_unseen(self) -> frozenset[str]:
    """
    The names of `refinery.lib.scripts.js.keys.STRINGS_READ_ANYWHERE` whose readers answer no
    for this program, so that a string spelling one anywhere would change an answer: one
    spelling `constructor` lets a constructor escape (`constructors_may_escape`), and one
    spelling `caller` may read the function that called another (`spells_caller`).
    """
    unseen: set[str] = set()
    if not self.constructors_may_escape():
        unseen.add('constructor')
    if not self.spells_caller():
        unseen.add('caller')
    return frozenset(unseen)
def has_opaque_global_write(self)

Whether the program stores a property on the global object under a key only the runtime resolves (window[expr] = x), or installs one there under such a key (opaque_global_write), so an intrinsic or a script-scope name may hold something else than what the text spells once the program runs. The read-naming question has_reflection_surface answers is unaffected by such a write — storing a property names nothing and runs nothing — but the replacement questions are not: a written key may be Math, String, or the name a top-level var carries, so a consumer that trusts an intrinsic by name, or that a script-scope binding keeps its spelled value, refuses while this holds.

Detection is model-aware, distinct from the spelling-level exemption _is_reflective_member grants the same sites: a base may be the global object here (may_be_the_global_object), so a local holding the object (var g = globalThis; g[k] = 1<code>) and the receiver a sloppy call supplies a write through (</code>this[k] = 1) are both counted, not only its spelled names — the alias would otherwise store a global under a key the spelling never saw, and the receiver is the one spelling a callee can choose freely. Every store form counts (is_member_write_target: plain and compound assignment, update, delete, for-in/for-of heads, destructuring patterns), so the fact stands on its own wherever a consumer consults it. Computed lazily and memoized, but never while _build_def_use is still recording references: which member accesses reach the global object depends on the names holding it, which that step finds from the writes it records, so an answer taken inside it would depend on how far it had got; asked there, the conservative True is answered instead of a partial fact.

Expand source code Browse git 
def has_opaque_global_write(self) -> bool:
    """
    Whether the program stores a property on the global object under a key only the runtime
    resolves (`window[expr] = x`), or installs one there under such a key
    (`opaque_global_write`), so an intrinsic or a script-scope name may hold something else
    than what the text spells once the program runs. The read-naming question
    `has_reflection_surface` answers is unaffected by such a write — storing a property names
    nothing and runs nothing — but the *replacement* questions are not: a written key may be
    `Math`, `String`, or the name a top-level `var` carries, so a consumer that trusts an
    intrinsic by name, or that a script-scope binding keeps its spelled value, refuses while
    this holds.

    Detection is model-aware, distinct from the spelling-level exemption
    `_is_reflective_member` grants the same sites: a base may be the global object here
    (`may_be_the_global_object`), so a local holding the object (`var g = globalThis;
    g[k] = 1`) and the receiver a sloppy call supplies a write through (`this[k] = 1`) are
    both counted, not only its spelled names — the alias would otherwise store a global under
    a key the spelling never saw, and the receiver is the one spelling a callee can choose
    freely. Every store form counts
    (`is_member_write_target`: plain and compound assignment, update, `delete`, `for-in`/`for-of`
    heads, destructuring patterns), so the fact stands on its own wherever a consumer consults it.
    Computed lazily and memoized,
    but never while `_build_def_use` is still recording references: which member accesses reach
    the global object depends on the names holding it, which that step finds from the writes it
    records, so an answer taken inside it would depend on how far it had got; asked there, the
    conservative `True` is answered instead of a partial fact.
    """
    if self._recording_def_use:
        return True
    if not self._opaque_global_write_sites_known:
        self._compute_opaque_global_write_sites()
    return self._opaque_global_write is True
def opaque_global_write_sites(self)

The member expressions storing a property on the global object under a key only the runtime resolves — the located form of the fact has_opaque_global_write reports, for a consumer that orders the fact's consequences rather than refusing on it. None when the fact holds without a site to order: an observed hand-over of the object to a callee that may write it, or an answer taken while the reference-recording walk is still running, where the boolean answers True for the same reason. A consumer that turns sites into kills treats None as volatility it cannot locate.

Expand source code Browse git 
def opaque_global_write_sites(self) -> list[JsMemberExpression] | None:
    """
    The member expressions storing a property on the global object under a key only the runtime
    resolves — the located form of the fact `has_opaque_global_write` reports, for a consumer
    that orders the fact's consequences rather than refusing on it. `None` when the fact holds
    without a site to order: an observed hand-over of the object to a callee that may write it,
    or an answer taken while the reference-recording walk is still running, where the boolean
    answers `True` for the same reason. A consumer that turns sites into kills treats `None` as
    volatility it cannot locate.
    """
    if self._recording_def_use:
        return None
    if not self._opaque_global_write_sites_known:
        self._compute_opaque_global_write_sites()
    sites = self._opaque_global_write_sites
    assert sites is not None
    if sites or self._opaque_global_write is not True:
        return sites
    return None
def opaque_global_write_replacement_sites(self, binding)

The opaque global writes that could replace the value binding holds, or None when that question cannot be answered in sites. Only a property of the global object is replaceable, in some way the file may run (_global_object_may_hold), so any other binding answers None: its reflection hazards, if any, are not this write's. Such a property under a reflection surface answers None too, since any surface could write the name from anywhere, and so does the fact when it holds without a site (opaque_global_write_sites). None therefore means the binding is reflection-reachable for reasons no located site spells, and a consumer that turned sites into kills keeps the value volatile instead.

Expand source code Browse git 
def opaque_global_write_replacement_sites(self, binding: Binding) -> list[JsMemberExpression] | None:
    """
    The opaque global writes that could replace the value *binding* holds, or `None` when that
    question cannot be answered in sites. Only a property of the global object is replaceable,
    in some way the file may run (`_global_object_may_hold`), so any other binding answers
    `None`: its reflection hazards, if any, are not this write's. Such a property under a
    reflection surface answers `None` too, since any surface could write the name from anywhere,
    and so does the fact when it holds without a site (`opaque_global_write_sites`). `None`
    therefore means the binding is reflection-reachable for reasons no located site spells, and
    a consumer that turned sites into kills keeps the value volatile instead.
    """
    if not self._global_object_may_hold(binding) or self.has_reflection_surface():
        return None
    return self.opaque_global_write_sites()
def reflection_can_reach(self, binding)

Whether a runtime name lookup could read or write binding without a reference this model records. Derived over the precise dynamic-scope facts. A global is reachable through any reflective surface — eval, Function, a string timer, dynamic global access, with — all of which run in the global scope, so it defers to the whole-program has_reflection_surface, and, where it is a property of the global object (_global_object_may_hold), by an opaque global write rebinding its name (has_opaque_global_write), which no reference records either. A function-local is reachable only from within its own function and only by name: a with body that names it (a dynamic_references entry), a direct eval in the function (local_reachable_by_direct_eval), or a span of the function this model never read (unread_source_can_reach), which may spell the name where nothing records that it does. A with that never names it cannot reach it, and reflective code in the global scope cannot name a local — so the local answer is exact, while the global one stays conservative (any surface).

Where the file is a module of either kind a declaration of the top-level scope is scoped to the file: global-scope code cannot name it, so it is reached the way a function-local is, from the file's own text only (reflection_can_reach_from_its_scope). An implicit global stays a global.

A function declared in a block is reached wherever the var Annex B copies it into is (AnnexBCopy.observed): code run from a string at the top of a script calls such a function through the copy, never through the binding of the block.

Code a string spells is read where the trusting model reads it (spelled_code_sites), and reaches exactly the names it uses (named_by_spelled_code).

Expand source code Browse git 
def reflection_can_reach(self, binding: Binding) -> bool:
    """
    Whether a runtime name lookup could read or write *binding* without a reference this model
    records. Derived over the precise dynamic-scope facts. A global is reachable through any
    reflective surface — `eval`, `Function`, a string timer, dynamic global access, `with` — all
    of which run in the global scope, so it defers to the whole-program
    `has_reflection_surface`, and, where it is a property of the global object
    (`_global_object_may_hold`), by an opaque global write rebinding its name
    (`has_opaque_global_write`), which no reference records either. A function-local is
    reachable only from within its own function and only by name: a `with` body that names
    it (a `dynamic_references` entry), a direct `eval` in the function
    (`local_reachable_by_direct_eval`), or a span of the function this model never read
    (`unread_source_can_reach`), which may spell the name where nothing records that it does.
    A `with` that never names it cannot reach it, and reflective code in the global scope
    cannot name a local — so the local answer is exact, while the global one stays
    conservative (any surface).

    Where the file is a module of either kind a declaration of the top-level scope is scoped to
    the file: global-scope code cannot name it, so it is reached the way a function-local is,
    from the file's own text only (`reflection_can_reach_from_its_scope`). An implicit global
    stays a global.

    A function declared in a block is reached wherever the `var` Annex B copies it into is
    (`AnnexBCopy.observed`): code run from a string at the top of a script calls such a
    function through the copy, never through the binding of the block.

    Code a string spells is read where the trusting model reads it (`spelled_code_sites`), and
    reaches exactly the names it uses (`named_by_spelled_code`).
    """
    return _itself_or_its_annex_b_copy(binding, self._reflection_can_name)
def spelled_code_sites(self)

Every call of the program that runs code a string spells, as the trusting model reads it (_spelled_code_at). The other model reads no such code: it takes every call that runs a string for a reflective surface. Memoized; while the model is built, the memo is dropped after each step that records writes the recognizer of Function reads (_forget_spelled_code_sites), so that the steps asking in between walk the tree once.

Expand source code Browse git 
def spelled_code_sites(self) -> list[SpelledCodeSite]:
    """
    Every call of the program that runs code a string spells, as the trusting model reads it
    (`_spelled_code_at`). The other model reads no such code: it takes every call that runs a
    string for a reflective surface. Memoized; while the model is built, the memo is dropped
    after each step that records writes the recognizer of `Function` reads
    (`_forget_spelled_code_sites`), so that the steps asking in between walk the tree once.
    """
    if self._spelled_code_sites is not None:
        return self._spelled_code_sites
    sites: list[SpelledCodeSite] = []
    if self.trust_eval:
        for node in self.root.walk():
            if isinstance(node, (JsCallExpression, JsNewExpression)):
                site = self._spelled_code_at(node)
                if site is not None:
                    sites.append(site)
    self._spelled_code_sites = sites
    return sites
def trusts_the_code_at(self, call)

Whether the trusting model takes the direct eval call for one that names nothing: it trusts code it cannot read, and reads the text of an eval the program spells (_spelled_text_at) as it reads the file's own. An eval handed no argument runs nothing.

Expand source code Browse git 
def trusts_the_code_at(self, call: Node) -> bool:
    """
    Whether the trusting model takes the direct `eval` *call* for one that names nothing: it
    trusts code it cannot read, and reads the text of an `eval` the program spells
    (`_spelled_text_at`) as it reads the file's own. An `eval` handed no argument runs nothing.
    """
    if not self.trust_eval or not isinstance(call, JsCallExpression):
        return False
    texts = call.arguments[:1]
    return not texts or self._spelled_text_at(texts[0]) is None
def names_its_scope(self, node)

Whether node is a direct eval (is_direct_eval_call) this model takes for one that may name every binding in scope where it runs: each one under the suspecting model, and under the trusting model each one it does not trust (trusts_the_code_at).

Expand source code Browse git 
def names_its_scope(self, node: Node) -> bool:
    """
    Whether *node* is a direct `eval` (`is_direct_eval_call`) this model takes for one that may
    name every binding in scope where it runs: each one under the suspecting model, and under
    the trusting model each one it does not trust (`trusts_the_code_at`).
    """
    return is_direct_eval_call(node) and not self.trusts_the_code_at(node)

def named_by_spelled_code(self, binding: Binding) -> bool:
    """
    Whether code a string spells names *binding* (`spelled_code_sites`), which no reference of
    this model records, or names the `var` Annex B copies it into, through which code outside
